========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… the main reason I came in is that my thinking just hasn’t felt right. It’s like my mind is full of fog, if that makes sense. I work around cataloguing notes and clerical things at the library, and I used to be able to follow my old notes, keep my place, know what came next — nothing fancy, just the usual steps — but lately I’ll be looking right at them and still lose track of where I am.

Even simple things feel harder to decide. I’ll stop and think, “What am I supposed to do next?” and it’s something I should know. It’s embarrassing, really, because these are not complicated decisions. I just feel slowed down and muddled, like I can’t get my thoughts lined up properly. That’s what finally made me think I should come talk to someone.

It’s been… I’d say about three months now, most days. It didn’t feel like one clear day where everything changed, but looking back, that’s about when it started to really settle in.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I have. Most days lately, I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table and it just feels like the color has gone out of everything. That’s the best way I can put it. Not crying all the time or anything like that, necessarily, but just this heaviness, like everything is dull and flat.

Even my old watercolor books — I used to enjoy looking through them, just the colors and the little techniques, the way the pages were laid out — now I’ll open one and it doesn’t lift me at all. If anything, it makes me feel heavier, because I notice that I’m not interested the way I used to be. So yes, I’d say I’ve been feeling pretty sad or depressed most of the time.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… there are stretches when it feels worse, definitely. I mean, most days have been low, like I said, but sometimes it settles on me more heavily. I’ll just end up sitting at the kitchen table for a long time, not really doing anything, and it honestly feels like all the color has gone out of the room, out of the day — out of everything, really.

And the thing that bothers me is, even the little things that used to give me some comfort don’t really do that now. I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m reminded that I don’t feel the way I used to. So yes, I’d say there have been times over these months where the sadness feels more intense.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I have. I used to get real pleasure from little things, especially my watercolor books. Not even necessarily painting — just sitting with them, looking at the colors, the paper, the way the examples were done. That used to calm me down or at least make me feel interested in something.

Lately, though, I’ll sit at the kitchen table and open one, and it’s like… nothing comes back. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.

The watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.

So… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.

Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.

Most days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.

But now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.

I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.

My wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.

So I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.

But it hasn’t just been an occasional off day. Most days lately, I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table and it just feels like the color has gone out of everything. That’s the best way I can put it. Not crying all the time or anything like that, necessarily, but just this heaviness, like everything is dull and flat.\n\nEven my old watercolor books — I used to enjoy looking through them, just the colors and the little techniques, the way the pages were laid out — now I’ll open one and it doesn’t lift me at all. If anything, it makes me feel heavier, because I notice that I’m not interested the way I used to be. I mean, most days have been low, like I said, but sometimes it settles on me more heavily. I’ll just end up sitting at the kitchen table for a long time, not really doing anything, and it honestly feels like all the color has gone out of the room, out of the day — out of everything, really.\n\nAnd the thing that bothers me is, even the little things that used to give me some comfort don’t really do that now. I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m reminded that I don’t feel the way I used to. I used to get real pleasure from little things, especially my watercolor books. Not even necessarily painting — just sitting with them, looking at the colors, the paper, the way the examples were done. That used to calm me down or at least make me feel interested in something.\n\nLately, though, I’ll sit at the kitchen table and open one, and it’s like… nothing comes back. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.

Even just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.

It’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.

Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.

So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.\n\nI do get this vague feeling sometimes, like I’m somehow not doing things right or I’m letting people down, but when I try to point to what exactly I mean, it gets kind of slippery. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.

I mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.

It’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… the main reason I came in is that my thinking just hasn’t felt right. It’s like my mind is full of fog, if that makes sense. I work around cataloguing notes and clerical things at the library, and I used to be able to follow my old notes, keep my place, know what came next — nothing fancy, just the usual steps — but lately I’ll be looking right at them and still lose track of where I am.\n\nEven simple things feel harder to decide. I’ll stop and think, “What am I supposed to do next?” and it’s something I should know. It’s embarrassing, really, because these are not complicated decisions. I just feel slowed down and muddled, like I can’t get my thoughts lined up properly. That’s what finally made me think I should come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with your mind feeling foggy and having trouble keeping track of things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about three months now, most days. It didn’t feel like one clear day where everything changed, but looking back, that’s about when it started to really settle in.\n\nIt was around the time I began getting ready to leave the library, and I think that’s why I kept telling myself it was just stress or being tired from all the preparations and changes. But it hasn’t just been an occasional off day. The low mood and the foggy thinking have been there more often than not, day after day, for those three months or so.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started experiencing the foggy thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. Most days lately, I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table and it just feels like the color has gone out of everything. That’s the best way I can put it. Not crying all the time or anything like that, necessarily, but just this heaviness, like everything is dull and flat.\n\nEven my old watercolor books — I used to enjoy looking through them, just the colors and the little techniques, the way the pages were laid out — now I’ll open one and it doesn’t lift me at all. If anything, it makes me feel heavier, because I notice that I’m not interested the way I used to be. So yes, I’d say I’ve been feeling pretty sad or depressed most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even more intense sadness or depression over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… there are stretches when it feels worse, definitely. I mean, most days have been low, like I said, but sometimes it settles on me more heavily. I’ll just end up sitting at the kitchen table for a long time, not really doing anything, and it honestly feels like all the color has gone out of the room, out of the day — out of everything, really.\n\nAnd the thing that bothers me is, even the little things that used to give me some comfort don’t really do that now. I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m reminded that I don’t feel the way I used to. So yes, I’d say there have been times over these months where the sadness feels more intense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I used to get real pleasure from little things, especially my watercolor books. Not even necessarily painting — just sitting with them, looking at the colors, the paper, the way the examples were done. That used to calm me down or at least make me feel interested in something.\n\nLately, though, I’ll sit at the kitchen table and open one, and it’s like… nothing comes back. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.\n\nI do get this vague feeling sometimes, like I’m somehow not doing things right or I’m letting people down, but when I try to point to what exactly I mean, it gets kind of slippery. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.\n\nI mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nIt’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F32.0", "F32.1"], "note": "", "is_final": false}

Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.\n\nI mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nIt’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of your daily needs or make decisions for yourself?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / library_assistant_clerical / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Diminished_Thinking\nmanifestation: \"I came in because my mind feels foggy, and I cannot even keep my place in my old cataloguing notes or decide what simple thing to do next.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I wouldn’t say I’ve been completely unable to take care of myself, like I can’t feed myself or anything like that. But the decision-making part… yes, that’s been a real problem, and it’s actually a big reason I came in.

My mind just feels foggy. That’s the best word I can find for it. I’ll be at work with my old cataloguing notes — notes I’ve used for years, things that should be familiar to me — and I can’t keep my place. I’ll read the same line and then lose where I was, or I’ll look at the next thing I’m supposed to do and it’s like my brain won’t move forward.

And then even simple decisions feel strangely hard. Not big life decisions, I mean something as plain as, “Do I start with this stack or that one?” or “What should I do next?” I’ll sit there turning it over like it’s complicated, when I know it shouldn’t be. It makes me feel useless, honestly, because these are ordinary things I used to handle without thinking.

So, completely unable to care for myself — no, not completely. But unable to think clearly enough to decide the next simple step? That’s been happening, and it’s frightening in its own quiet way.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… the main reason I came in is that my thinking just hasn’t felt right. It’s like my mind is full of fog, if that makes sense. I work around cataloguing notes and clerical things at the library, and I used to be able to follow my old notes, keep my place, know what came next — nothing fancy, just the usual steps — but lately I’ll be looking right at them and still lose track of where I am.\n\nEven simple things feel harder to decide. I’ll stop and think, “What am I supposed to do next?” and it’s something I should know. It’s embarrassing, really, because these are not complicated decisions. I just feel slowed down and muddled, like I can’t get my thoughts lined up properly. That’s what finally made me think I should come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with your mind feeling foggy and having trouble keeping track of things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about three months now, most days. It didn’t feel like one clear day where everything changed, but looking back, that’s about when it started to really settle in.\n\nIt was around the time I began getting ready to leave the library, and I think that’s why I kept telling myself it was just stress or being tired from all the preparations and changes. But it hasn’t just been an occasional off day. The low mood and the foggy thinking have been there more often than not, day after day, for those three months or so.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started experiencing the foggy thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. Most days lately, I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table and it just feels like the color has gone out of everything. That’s the best way I can put it. Not crying all the time or anything like that, necessarily, but just this heaviness, like everything is dull and flat.\n\nEven my old watercolor books — I used to enjoy looking through them, just the colors and the little techniques, the way the pages were laid out — now I’ll open one and it doesn’t lift me at all. If anything, it makes me feel heavier, because I notice that I’m not interested the way I used to be. So yes, I’d say I’ve been feeling pretty sad or depressed most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even more intense sadness or depression over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… there are stretches when it feels worse, definitely. I mean, most days have been low, like I said, but sometimes it settles on me more heavily. I’ll just end up sitting at the kitchen table for a long time, not really doing anything, and it honestly feels like all the color has gone out of the room, out of the day — out of everything, really.\n\nAnd the thing that bothers me is, even the little things that used to give me some comfort don’t really do that now. I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m reminded that I don’t feel the way I used to. So yes, I’d say there have been times over these months where the sadness feels more intense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I used to get real pleasure from little things, especially my watercolor books. Not even necessarily painting — just sitting with them, looking at the colors, the paper, the way the examples were done. That used to calm me down or at least make me feel interested in something.\n\nLately, though, I’ll sit at the kitchen table and open one, and it’s like… nothing comes back. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to measure it exactly, because it all sort of blends together after a while, but there have been days lately where it’s felt noticeably worse than just my usual low mood.

Most days now, I find myself sitting at the kitchen table, sometimes for longer than I mean to, and it feels like the color has gone out of everything. That sounds a little dramatic, maybe, but that’s honestly how it feels. Things are still there — the light coming in, the dishes, my books, all the ordinary things — but they don’t seem to reach me the same way.

I used to look through my old watercolor books, even if I wasn’t painting anything, just to enjoy the colors and the paper and the way the artists handled light. Lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested or comforted, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m looking at something I know should matter to me, but I can’t quite feel it. And on the worse days, that heaviness is stronger — I just sit there with it.

So, yes, I’d say there have been periods where it’s been worse than the average, though I’m not sure I could draw a neat line around them.

Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.\n\nI do get this vague feeling sometimes, like I’m somehow not doing things right or I’m letting people down, but when I try to point to what exactly I mean, it gets kind of slippery. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.\n\nI mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nIt’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of your daily needs or make decisions for yourself?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve been completely unable to take care of myself, like I can’t feed myself or anything like that. But the decision-making part… yes, that’s been a real problem, and it’s actually a big reason I came in.\n\nMy mind just feels foggy. That’s the best word I can find for it. I’ll be at work with my old cataloguing notes — notes I’ve used for years, things that should be familiar to me — and I can’t keep my place. I’ll read the same line and then lose where I was, or I’ll look at the next thing I’m supposed to do and it’s like my brain won’t move forward.\n\nAnd then even simple decisions feel strangely hard. Not big life decisions, I mean something as plain as, “Do I start with this stack or that one?” or “What should I do next?” I’ll sit there turning it over like it’s complicated, when I know it shouldn’t be. It makes me feel useless, honestly, because these are ordinary things I used to handle without thinking.\n\nSo, completely unable to care for myself — no, not completely. But unable to think clearly enough to decide the next simple step? That’s been happening, and it’s frightening in its own quiet way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your low mood was significantly worse than your average low mood over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to measure it exactly, because it all sort of blends together after a while, but there have been days lately where it’s felt noticeably worse than just my usual low mood.\n\nMost days now, I find myself sitting at the kitchen table, sometimes for longer than I mean to, and it feels like the color has gone out of everything. That sounds a little dramatic, maybe, but that’s honestly how it feels. Things are still there — the light coming in, the dishes, my books, all the ordinary things — but they don’t seem to reach me the same way.\n\nI used to look through my old watercolor books, even if I wasn’t painting anything, just to enjoy the colors and the paper and the way the artists handled light. Lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested or comforted, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m looking at something I know should matter to me, but I can’t quite feel it. And on the worse days, that heaviness is stronger — I just sit there with it.\n\nSo, yes, I’d say there have been periods where it’s been worse than the average, though I’m not sure I could draw a neat line around them.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think I’ve had many days where I literally could not get out of bed at all, if you mean I was completely stuck there. I do get up, eventually. But everything feels so slowed down, like my body is moving through something thick.

Even little things take more effort than they should. Walking to the corner store, which isn’t far at all, can feel like a whole undertaking. I’ll stand there thinking, “It’s just down the street, why does this feel like such a project?” And at my desk, even sorting papers or moving things into piles feels strangely heavy and slow, like my hands and my mind aren’t keeping pace with what I’m trying to do.

So, not unable in the absolute sense, no. I can still do the simplest tasks, but they don’t feel simple. They feel like they take a lot more energy and time than they used to.

I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.\n\nI do get this vague feeling sometimes, like I’m somehow not doing things right or I’m letting people down, but when I try to point to what exactly I mean, it gets kind of slippery. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.\n\nI mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nIt’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of your daily needs or make decisions for yourself?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve been completely unable to take care of myself, like I can’t feed myself or anything like that. But the decision-making part… yes, that’s been a real problem, and it’s actually a big reason I came in.\n\nMy mind just feels foggy. That’s the best word I can find for it. I’ll be at work with my old cataloguing notes — notes I’ve used for years, things that should be familiar to me — and I can’t keep my place. I’ll read the same line and then lose where I was, or I’ll look at the next thing I’m supposed to do and it’s like my brain won’t move forward.\n\nAnd then even simple decisions feel strangely hard. Not big life decisions, I mean something as plain as, “Do I start with this stack or that one?” or “What should I do next?” I’ll sit there turning it over like it’s complicated, when I know it shouldn’t be. It makes me feel useless, honestly, because these are ordinary things I used to handle without thinking.\n\nSo, completely unable to care for myself — no, not completely. But unable to think clearly enough to decide the next simple step? That’s been happening, and it’s frightening in its own quiet way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your low mood was significantly worse than your average low mood over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s hard for me to measure it exactly, because it all sort of blends together after a while, but there have been days lately where it’s felt noticeably worse than just my usual low mood.\n\nMost days now, I find myself sitting at the kitchen table, sometimes for longer than I mean to, and it feels like the color has gone out of everything. That sounds a little dramatic, maybe, but that’s honestly how it feels. Things are still there — the light coming in, the dishes, my books, all the ordinary things — but they don’t seem to reach me the same way.\n\nI used to look through my old watercolor books, even if I wasn’t painting anything, just to enjoy the colors and the paper and the way the artists handled light. Lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested or comforted, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m looking at something I know should matter to me, but I can’t quite feel it. And on the worse days, that heaviness is stronger — I just sit there with it.\n\nSo, yes, I’d say there have been periods where it’s been worse than the average, though I’m not sure I could draw a neat line around them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past three months where you felt so low that you couldn't get out of bed or perform even the simplest tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had many days where I literally could not get out of bed at all, if you mean I was completely stuck there. I do get up, eventually. But everything feels so slowed down, like my body is moving through something thick.\n\nEven little things take more effort than they should. Walking to the corner store, which isn’t far at all, can feel like a whole undertaking. I’ll stand there thinking, “It’s just down the street, why does this feel like such a project?” And at my desk, even sorting papers or moving things into piles feels strangely heavy and slow, like my hands and my mind aren’t keeping pace with what I’m trying to do.\n\nSo, not unable in the absolute sense, no. I can still do the simplest tasks, but they don’t feel simple. They feel like they take a lot more energy and time than they used to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think that’s fair to say. Not in the sense that I’m lying in bed the entire day doing absolutely nothing, but there are definitely days where the low mood seems to get into almost everything I try to do.

The clearest example is how much I’ve let things pile up. I have lecture plans I meant to organize, travel notes I started and then just left half-sorted, and even ordinary household paperwork — bills, forms, little bits of mail that should take ten minutes — all of it has been sitting there because I can’t reliably pull myself together and organize it the way I used to.

It’s embarrassing, actually, because these are exactly the kinds of things I used to be good at. I’ve always been the person who could make a folder, label things, keep track of notes, make a clean list. But lately I’ll sit down to do it and I just sort of stare at the papers. I move one pile from one side of the table to the other, then I’m not sure what I was doing, and then I give up because it feels like I’m making it worse.

So yes, on those days it interferes with nearly everything practical. I may still be moving around, making tea, answering a message here or there, but the real tasks of the day — the things that need planning or sorting or following through — they just don’t happen.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… the main reason I came in is that my thinking just hasn’t felt right. It’s like my mind is full of fog, if that makes sense. I’ll stop and think, “What am I supposed to do next?” and it’s something I should know. It’s embarrassing, really, because these are not complicated decisions. I just feel slowed down and muddled, like I can’t get my thoughts lined up properly. That’s what finally made me think I should come talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with your mind feeling foggy and having trouble keeping track of things?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I’d say about three months now, most days. It didn’t feel like one clear day where everything changed, but looking back, that’s about when it started to really settle in.\n\nIt was around the time I began getting ready to leave the library, and I think that’s why I kept telling myself it was just stress or being tired from all the preparations and changes. But it hasn’t just been an occasional off day. The low mood and the foggy thinking have been there more often than not, day after day, for those three months or so.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed most of the time since you started experiencing the foggy thinking?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have. Most days lately, I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table and it just feels like the color has gone out of everything. That’s the best way I can put it. Not crying all the time or anything like that, necessarily, but just this heaviness, like everything is dull and flat.\n\nEven my old watercolor books — I used to enjoy looking through them, just the colors and the little techniques, the way the pages were laid out — now I’ll open one and it doesn’t lift me at all. If anything, it makes me feel heavier, because I notice that I’m not interested the way I used to be. So yes, I’d say I’ve been feeling pretty sad or depressed most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even more intense sadness or depression over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… there are stretches when it feels worse, definitely. I mean, most days have been low, like I said, but sometimes it settles on me more heavily. I’ll just end up sitting at the kitchen table for a long time, not really doing anything, and it honestly feels like all the color has gone out of the room, out of the day — out of everything, really.\n\nAnd the thing that bothers me is, even the little things that used to give me some comfort don’t really do that now. I have these old watercolor books I used to like looking through — not even painting, necessarily, just looking at the colors and the examples — and lately I’ll open one and instead of feeling interested, it just makes me feel heavy. Like I’m reminded that I don’t feel the way I used to. So yes, I’d say there have been times over these months where the sadness feels more intense.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I used to get real pleasure from little things, especially my watercolor books. Not even necessarily painting — just sitting with them, looking at the colors, the paper, the way the examples were done. That used to calm me down or at least make me feel interested in something.\n\nLately, though, I’ll sit at the kitchen table and open one, and it’s like… nothing comes back. Or worse, it makes me feel heavy, because I can tell I’m not interested the way I used to be. Most days it feels like the color has gone out of everything, which is a strange thing to say when I’m looking right at pages full of color, but that’s how it feels. So yes, I’ve definitely lost interest.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much. I keep trying to think of something, because I don’t want to make it sound like everything is completely gone, but most days lately I just end up at the kitchen table with that same feeling, like the color has gone out of everything.\n\nThe watercolor books are the clearest example, because those used to be something I could count on. I’d flip through them and sort of enjoy the colors or the little details, even if I didn’t do anything with it. Now I’ll open one and it doesn’t give me that little lift. It just makes me feel heavy, like I’m looking at something that used to matter to me and I can’t quite reach it anymore.\n\nSo… no, I can’t really say there’s much I look forward to right now. Maybe I still do things out of habit, but pleasure? Not really.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to experience pleasure in things that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s exactly what it feels like. Like the part of me that used to take something in and feel a little bit of pleasure from it just isn’t working right now.\n\nMost days lately, I end up sitting at the kitchen table, and everything feels sort of drained out — I keep saying it feels like the color has gone out of everything, but that really is the closest way I can describe it. And it’s strange because the watercolor books I used to love are right there. Those were the sort of thing that normally would bring me some happiness, or at least interest. I’d look through them and notice the shades and the paper and all the little details.\n\nBut now when I open them, I don’t get that feeling. If anything, it just makes me feel heavier, because I can tell I’m supposed to enjoy it, or I remember enjoying it, but I can’t seem to reach that feeling anymore. So yes… I’d say I’ve lost that ability, at least lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past three months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number. What I have noticed is more around eating. I have to sort of remind myself to eat now, which isn’t really like me.\n\nMy wife has noticed it too. She’ll point out that I leave rice and soup half-finished, and before, I would never waste a meal like that. I was raised not to, for one thing, but also I just used to finish what was in front of me without thinking about it. Lately I’ll start eating and then just… stop. Not because I’m trying to cut back or anything, it just doesn’t seem to draw me in, and then the bowl sits there.\n\nSo I’m not sure exactly how much weight has changed, but my appetite definitely has, and enough that it’s noticeable at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I have noticed that. It’s like my body has gotten slow in a way I can’t quite explain. Not like I’m sore or injured, exactly, but everything takes more effort than it should.\n\nEven just walking to the corner store feels like this whole undertaking now. I’ll think, “It’s only a few blocks, just go,” but getting myself up and out the door feels heavy, and then when I’m walking, I feel like I’m moving through something thick. And at my desk, even sorting papers — which is such a basic thing, I’ve done that kind of work for years — it feels harder to start. I’ll sit there looking at a stack and know exactly what needs doing, but my hands don’t seem to want to begin.\n\nIt’s embarrassing, honestly, because these aren’t big tasks. They’re ordinary little things. But lately my body feels slowed down, and getting started feels like pushing against a weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… I’m not sure I’d say there’s one particular thing, no. Not like I’ve done something specific and keep replaying it in my head.\n\nI do get this vague feeling sometimes, like I’m somehow not doing things right or I’m letting people down, but when I try to point to what exactly I mean, it gets kind of slippery. I can’t say, “Oh, it’s because of this one thing.” It’s more like a general heaviness or worry about not being myself, if that makes sense.\n\nBut guilty about something in particular… no, I don’t think I have a clear answer for that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide lately?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think I’ve been having thoughts like that. Not in the sense of wanting to die or thinking about suicide. That’s not something I’ve been planning or sitting with.\n\nI mean, I have had low moments where I feel worn down or discouraged, and sometimes I’ll think, “I don’t want to keep feeling this way,” but that’s different, at least to me. It’s more like wanting the heaviness to pass, not wanting my life to end. I don’t have a plan or anything like that.\n\nIt’s uncomfortable to talk about, but no — I haven’t been thinking about killing myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where you felt completely unable to take care of your daily needs or make decisions for yourself?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’ve been completely unable to take care of myself, like I can’t feed myself or anything like that.
